# BedAura | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/bedaura.md)
.

It can rotate to the bed, break a block covering it, and continue the break sequence while you deal with nearby players.

**Blatant setup**

Aggressive break settings are easier for server anti-cheat to detect. A high-speed setup uses `Break mode: Swap`, `Break speed: 1.12x`, and `Range: 4.5`. It also enables `Break near block`, `Ignore slow`, and `Maximize speed`, while leaving `Allow KillAura` and `Ground spoof` off. `Skip break verification` is optional and can help high-ping players.

Find the bed[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/bedaura#find-the-bed)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Range` sets how far away BedAura can target a bed. `4.5` blocks matches vanilla block interaction reach. `FOV` limits targeting to the angle around the direction you face.

With `Only while visible` enabled, BedAura targets a bed only when you have line of sight. `Break near block` also lets it target a covered bed. It breaks the block closest to the bed first, so enable it when the bed is inside a defense.

Control the break[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/bedaura#control-the-break)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Break mode` chooses the break sequence:

*   `Legit` uses a standard mining sequence.
    
*   `Instant` breaks as fast as possible.
    
*   `Swap` changes to the optimal tool during the break.
    

`Break speed` multiplies the block-breaking speed. `1x` is vanilla speed, and higher values shorten the break. `Rate` controls how often BedAura sends break packets during a tick.

`Ignore slow` keeps the break speed from being reduced by a Slowness effect. `Maximize speed` selects the fastest break sequence available. `Skip break verification` skips the check for each break packet, which can reduce waiting when your connection has high ping.

Keep the break from being interrupted[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/bedaura#keep-the-break-from-being-interrupted)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Allow KillAura` lets KillAura run while BedAura is active. If both modules are enabled, `Prioritize KillAura` gives KillAura control before the bed break.

`Cancel knockback` suppresses incoming knockback while BedAura is breaking. `Disable bhop` turns off Bhop during the break. `Ground spoof` reports that you are on the ground while jumping, which prevents the break rate from being reduced in the air.

`Disable right click` blocks right-click actions while BedAura runs. It is enabled by default.

Show or hide the break[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/bedaura#show-or-hide-the-break)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Render block outline` highlights the bed or covering block. `Send animations` sends arm-swing animations with break actions. Both are enabled by default.

`Disable break effects` hides the particles and sounds from block breaking. `Silent swing` sends the swing without showing the arm animation.

Related modules[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/bedaura#related-modules)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [AutoBlockin](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/autoblockin)
    
*   [KillAura](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/killaura)
    

[PreviousAutoTool](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/autotool)
[NextDelay Remover](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/delay-remover)

# InvMove | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/invmove.md)
.

This lets you keep moving while you loot or manage items. A container is a screen such as a chest that holds items.

Movement in screens[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/invmove#movement-in-screens)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

InvMove uses one mode for your personal inventory and another for chests and other containers.

### Inventory[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/invmove#inventory)

`Inventory` controls movement while your personal inventory is open:

*   `Disabled` blocks movement.
    
*   `Vanilla` uses normal movement.
    
*   `Pause` sends movement packets with a delay.
    
*   `Blink` stores movement packets and sends them when the inventory closes.
    
*   `Close` closes the inventory when you move.
    

### Chest & others[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/invmove#chest-and-others)

`Chest & others` has the same choices except for `Close`:

*   `Disabled` blocks movement.
    
*   `Vanilla` uses normal movement.
    
*   `Pause` sends movement packets with a delay.
    
*   `Blink` stores movement packets and sends them when the container closes.
    

`Motion` limits movement speed while a screen is open. `1x` is full movement speed.

**Legit setup**

Set `Inventory` and `Chest & others` to `Pause`, set `Motion` to `1x`, and enable `Allow movement`. Leave the other movement permissions off. The delayed modes make the movement less abrupt than sending every packet at once.

Movement permissions[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/invmove#movement-permissions)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Allow movement` enables the `WASD` keys while a screen is open. The other permissions extend what you can do from there:

*   `Allow jumping` permits jumping.
    
*   `Allow sprinting` permits sprinting.
    
*   `Allow rotating` permits camera movement.
    
*   `Allow midair` keeps movement active while you are not on the ground.
    
*   `Allow speed potion` applies active Speed effects.
    

Corrections and activation[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/invmove#corrections-and-activation)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Only with inventory manager` runs InvMove only while InvManager is active. `Slow motion when necessary` reduces movement after the server sends a position correction. `Modify motion after click` adjusts movement packets after you click inside the inventory screen.

Related modules[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/invmove#related-modules)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [InvManager](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/invmanager)
    

[PreviousFly](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/fly)
[NextKeepSprint](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/keepsprint)

# Speed Builders | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/minigames/speed-builders.md)
.

You can use it as a guide or let it place matching blocks for you.

Start the helper[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/minigames/speed-builders#start-the-helper)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Activation` controls how you use the module. Choose `Toggle (Middle)` to turn it on or off with the middle mouse button. Choose `Hold (Right)` to keep it active while you hold the right mouse button.

Place and select blocks[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/minigames/speed-builders#place-and-select-blocks)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Auto place` has three modes:

*   `Disabled` leaves placement manual.
    
*   `Hover` places a block when you hover over its target position.
    
*   `Auto` places all matching blocks without requiring you to hover over each position.
    

`Place delay` sets the number of game ticks between placements. Its value can be set from `0` to `10` ticks.

`Auto swap` controls how Raven selects the block needed for the next placement:

*   `Disabled` does not change your selected block.
    
*   `Incremental` cycles to the next available block.
    
*   `Hover` changes to the needed block when you hover over its inventory slot.
    

Match the template[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/minigames/speed-builders#match-the-template)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Render blocks` controls the guide on the remaining positions:

*   `Disabled` hides the guide.
    
*   `All` highlights every remaining block.
    
*   `Holding` highlights blocks that match the block in your hand.
    
*   `Placeable` highlights only blocks you can place now.
    

Enable `Strict placements` to allow placement only in the same direction as the template.

Track the round[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/minigames/speed-builders#track-the-round)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Show stats` can be `Disabled`, `Chat`, or `Hover`. These choices hide the result, print it in chat, or show it when you hover.

Enable `Info HUD` to show current Speed Builders progress. `Show time` displays the remaining build time. `Save stats` keeps Speed Builders statistics between sessions.

# client | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/client.md)
.

see also: [`entity`](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/entity)
 for entity-specific methods and [`world`](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/world)
 for world data.

### addEnemy[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/client#addenemy)

adds the specified player to the enemy list.

Copy

    client.addEnemy(String username);

* * *

### addFriend[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/client#addfriend)

adds the specified player to the friend list.

Copy

    client.addFriend(String username);

* * *

### allowEditing[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/client#allowediting)

returns `true` if the player may edit blocks.

Copy

    boolean allowEditing = client.allowEditing();

* * *

### allowFlying[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/client#allowflying)

returns `true` if the player has the ability to fly.

Copy

    boolean allowFlying = client.allowFlying();

* * *

### attack[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/client#attack)

performs an attack on the specified [`Entity`](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/entity)
.

* * *

### async[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/client#async)

runs the provided task on a cached background thread.

* * *

### canSeeEntity[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/client#canseeentity)

returns `true` if the player has line of sight to the entity.

* * *

### chat[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/client#chat)

sends a chat message as the local player.

* * *

### closeScreen[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/client#closescreen)

closes the currently open gui.

* * *

### dropItem[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/client#dropitem)

drops the currently held item. set `stack` to `true` to drop the entire stack.

* * *

### getDisplaySize[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/client#getdisplaysize)

returns `[width, height, scaleFactor]` for the scaled window.

* * *

### getDirection[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/client#getdirection)

returns the movement direction string calculated by the client.

* * *

### getForward[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/client#getforward)

returns the forward movement input value.

* * *

### getFPS[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/client#getfps)

returns the current frames per second.

* * *

### getFreeMemory[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/client#getfreememory)

returns the amount of free jvm memory in bytes.

* * *

### getMaxMemory[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/client#getmaxmemory)

returns the maximum jvm memory allocation in bytes.

* * *

### getMotion[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/client#getmotion)

returns the local player's motion vector as a [`Vec3`](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/vec3)
.

* * *

### getPlayer[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/client#getplayer)

returns the local player as an [`Entity`](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/entity)
.

* * *

### getRenderArmPitch[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/client#getrenderarmpitch)

returns the render arm pitch angle.

* * *

### getRotations[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/client#getrotations)

returns `[yaw, pitch]` required to aim at a position.

* * *

### getRotationsToBlock[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/client#getrotationstoblock)

returns `[yaw, pitch]` needed to aim at a block. provide a face or omit to use the default face.

* * *

### getRotationsToEntity[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/client#getrotationstoentity)

returns `[yaw, pitch]` needed to aim at an entity.

* * *

### getScreen[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/client#getscreen)

* * *

makes the player jump.

# SafeWalk | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/safewalk.md)
.

It can move you back from the edge or use a sneak state, which makes it useful while bridging and when you need to stop at a ledge.

Choose how it protects the edge[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/safewalk#choose-how-it-protects-the-edge)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Mode` changes the edge behavior:

*   `Edge` moves your position away from the edge on the client.
    
*   `Sneak (Keybind)` simulates holding the sneak key.
    
*   `Sneak (State)` sets the sneak state directly.
    
*   `Scaffold` uses a mode intended for [Scaffold](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/scaffold)
    .
    

`Edge tolerance` controls how close you can get before SafeWalk acts. `Low`, `Medium`, `High`, and `Very High` are available. Higher tolerance values act earlier, which gives more room at the edge but can make the sneak happen too soon and look less natural.

`Minimum pitch` sets the minimum downward look angle. A value of `70` means SafeWalk only acts when you are looking well down toward the blocks.

**Legit setup**

Start with `Mode: Sneak (Keybind)`, `Minimum pitch: 70`, and `Edge tolerance: Medium`. `Very High` gives more room but is more noticeable. Enable `Blocks only` and `Disable on forward` to keep SafeWalk focused on bridging.

Limit when it can act[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/safewalk#limit-when-it-can-act)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Blocks only` makes SafeWalk protect you only while you hold a block. `Disable on forward` turns it off while you hold the forward key, so you can intentionally walk over an edge.

`Only while right clicking` limits SafeWalk to right-click actions. `Unsneak delay` sets how long it waits before releasing sneak after you move away from the edge.

Related modules[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/safewalk#related-modules)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [Scaffold](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/scaffold)
    
*   [Tower](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/tower)
    

[PreviousNoRotate](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/norotate)
[NextScaffold](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/scaffold)

# SlyPort | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/fun/slyport.md)
.

SlyPort is highly detectable. Treat it as a fun module.

Choose a target[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/fun/slyport#choose-a-target)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Range` controls how far away a target can be. Its value can be set from `2` to `15` blocks.

Enable `Players only` to target players and ignore mobs. Disable it if mobs should also be valid targets.

Finish the teleport[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/fun/slyport#finish-the-teleport)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Enable `Aim` to face the target after teleporting. `Play sound` adds a sound when SlyPort activates.

# Scaffold | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/scaffold.md)
.

It handles the placement timing and the rotation needed to build without looking down at every block.

**Blatant setup**

For the setup described here, use `Motion: 1x`, `Rotation: Smooth`, `Fast scaffold RMB: Keep-Y A` or `Jump B`, and `Multi-place: Off`. Enable `Auto swap`, `Jump facing forward`, and `Legit motion`. Leave `SafeWalk`, `Slow speed potion`, and `Slow on enable` off. `Tower` is optional. `Very High` precision can reduce FPS on a lower-end computer, and the `Jump B` fast scaffold variant can lead to quicker bans.

Control movement and rotation[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/scaffold#control-movement-and-rotation)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Motion` changes your movement speed while Scaffold is active. `1x` keeps normal walking speed. `Speed boost` adds a separate movement velocity multiplier. `Legit motion` adds small movement changes to the bridge movement.

`Rotation` chooses how Scaffold turns toward a placement position:

*   `None` keeps your current view direction.
    
*   `Strict` uses the exact placement angle.
    
*   `Smooth` changes the angle gradually.
    
*   `Simple` uses basic rotation.
    
*   `Lazy` uses less rotation.
    
*   `Offset` adds a small rotation offset.
    

`Rotation (fake)` controls rotation sent to the server while your visible camera stays still. `Off` sends no replacement rotation, `Packet` sends the placement rotation in a packet, and `Smooth` changes that sent rotation gradually.

Choose a fast scaffold pattern[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/scaffold#choose-a-fast-scaffold-pattern)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Fast scaffold` selects the movement pattern used for faster bridging. `Keep-Y A` and `Keep-Y B` keep your height with different timing variants. `Jump A` and `Jump B` use jump timing. `Telly A` through `Telly D` and `GodBridge A` through `GodBridge C` are named timing variants. `Breezily` and `StrictBreezily` are also available.

`Fast scaffold RMB` uses a pattern only while you hold right-click. Its choices are `Off`, `Keep-Y A`, `Jump A`, `Jump B`, `Telly`, and `GodBridge`. `Fast scaffold motion` changes the movement speed multiplier used by the selected pattern.

Place blocks safely[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/scaffold#place-blocks-safely)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Multi-place` controls whether Scaffold can place more than one block in a tick. `Off` places one block at a time, `On` allows multiple placements, and `Strict` allows them only when strict placement checks pass.

`Precision` controls how accurately Scaffold chooses a placement position. `Low`, `Medium`, `High`, and `Very High` are available. Higher precision can use more processing power.

Use `Very High` precision only if your computer can keep a stable frame rate. It can reduce FPS on lower-end hardware.

`Auto swap` changes to another block stack when the current one runs out. `Jump facing forward` allows jumps only when you face the bridge direction.

`SafeWalk` runs [SafeWalk](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/safewalk)
 with Scaffold. `Cancel knockback` suppresses incoming knockback while you bridge. `Silent swing` places blocks without showing the arm swing.

Show the placement[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/scaffold#show-the-placement)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Highlight blocks` marks the position of the next block. `Highlight theme` chooses the visual style for that mark. `Show block count` displays how many blocks remain.

`Tower` also enables [Tower](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/tower)
 while Scaffold is active. `Slow speed potion` reduces the speed boost while you have a Slowness effect. `Slow on enable` briefly reduces your speed when Scaffold starts.

Related modules[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/scaffold#related-modules)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [Tower](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/tower)
    
*   [FastPlace](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/fastplace)
    
*   [SafeWalk](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/safewalk)
    

[PreviousSafeWalk](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/safewalk)
[NextTower](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/tower)

# Lag Range | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/lag-range.md)
.

Players inside `Start range` use one delay, while players beyond it use another. You see an older position on your screen even though the server has the player's current position. A large buffer can make players look frozen or out of sync.

**Hypixel**

Hypixel currently eases burst movement packets in all game modes. Lag Range no longer gives its old teleport or hit advantage there and can cause ghosting. Disable Lag Range on Hypixel.

Split close and far delays[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/lag-range#split-close-and-far-delays)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Packet delay (close)` controls players inside `Start range`. `Packet delay (far)` controls players beyond that boundary. The two controls accept `10-800 ms`.

`Start range` sets the boundary between the two delays. It accepts `7-15 blocks`.

**Legit setup**

On a server where delayed positions still help, start with `Packet delay (close)` at `150 ms`, `Packet delay (far)` at `250 ms`, and `Max lag time` at `5 seconds`. If close players ghost, reduce or disable the close delay.

Limit and vary the buffer[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/lag-range#limit-and-vary-the-buffer)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Max lag time` caps how long a player's position can stay buffered. It accepts `0.5-15 seconds`. A `5 second` limit is a useful starting point.

`Random lag` varies the delay instead of using one fixed value. Leave it off for predictable timing, or turn it on when you want the delay to change.

`Weapon only` limits the delay to weapon-held situations. `Invisible players` includes invisible players in the buffer. `Ignore teammates` skips teammates. `Disable adventure` turns Lag Range off in Adventure mode.

Release positions before actions[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/lag-range#release-positions-before-actions)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The flush conditions release buffered positions before actions that can depend on current player locations. Enable the conditions that match the items you use:

*   `Holding bow`
    
*   `Holding fireball`
    
*   `Holding buckets`
    
*   `Holding fishing rod`
    
*   `Holding ladders`
    
*   `Holding any block`
    
*   `Interact item`
    

`Show server position` draws a ghost at each player's real server position while updates are buffered. Use it to check how far the displayed position has fallen behind.

If you want one recent position buffer instead of separate close and far delays, see [Backtrack](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/backtrack)
.

[PreviousFreeze](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/freeze)
[NextLimbo](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/limbo)

Last updated 1 day ago

*   [Split close and far delays](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/lag-range#split-close-and-far-delays)
    
*   [Limit and vary the buffer](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/lag-range#limit-and-vary-the-buffer)
    
*   [Release positions before actions](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/lag-range#release-positions-before-actions)

# Freeze | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/freeze.md)
.

While they are held, players can stop moving on your screen, blocks can stop changing, and time can stop advancing from your view. You can still move and act locally, but the server does not acknowledge those actions until the queued updates are released.

Stop and release incoming updates[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/freeze#stop-and-release-incoming-updates)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Auto-disable` turns Freeze off after the selected number of ticks. Set it to `0` to keep Freeze active until you disable it. The slider accepts `0-200 ticks`.

`Activate on hit` starts Freeze when you take damage. `Disable on bed break` releases the updates when a nearby bed breaks. `Disable on interact` releases them when you interact with a block.

Let selected updates through[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/freeze#let-selected-updates-through)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

These toggles allow one type of incoming update through while the rest stay frozen:

*   `Update block changes` keeps block events current.
    
*   `Update player health` keeps health updates current.
    
*   `Update player positions` keeps player movement current.
    
*   `Update chat messages` keeps chat current.
    
*   `Update scoreboard` keeps scoreboard updates current.
    
*   `Update sound effects` keeps sounds current.
    

See what is waiting[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/freeze#see-what-is-waiting)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Show freeze ticks` displays the number of queued ticks on the HUD. `Show knockback direction` draws an arrow for buffered knockback, which shows the direction of the movement that will arrive when the queue is released.

Freeze holds incoming packets from the server. [Blink](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/blink)
 holds outgoing movement packets from your client, so they affect opposite directions of traffic.

If you want a timed delay for your own outgoing packets, see [Fake Lag](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/fake-lag)
.

[PreviousFake Lag](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/fake-lag)

# NoSlow | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/noslow.md)
.

Blocking with a sword, drawing a bow, eating, and drinking normally slow you down. NoSlow lets you choose which of those actions should keep more movement.

Choose how the slowdown changes[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/noslow#choose-how-the-slowdown-changes)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### `Mode`[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/noslow#mode)

The `Mode` setting selects the packet method NoSlow uses. A packet is a message the client sends to the server.

*   `Vanilla` sends a release-use packet immediately.
    
*   `Blink` stores movement before use and sends it later.
    
*   `Ground` reports an on-ground state.
    
*   `Jump` jumps to interrupt the use-item state.
    
*   `Float` holds a small floating offset.
    
*   `Interact` sends an interact packet to cancel the slowdown.
    
*   `Invalid` sends an invalid action packet.
    
*   `Sneak` briefly uses sneak to cancel the slowdown.
    

Start with `Vanilla`. If the server does not accept that method, try `Interact`.

Choose what stays fast[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/noslow#choose-what-stays-fast)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Slow` controls how much of the normal penalty remains. `0%` removes it, while higher values keep more of it.

Use the item toggles to choose where NoSlow applies. `Sword` covers sword blocking, `Bow` covers drawing a bow, `Edibles` covers eating and drinking, and `Potions` covers drinking potions.

**Interact C autoblock**

If KillAura uses `Interact C` autoblock, enable `Sword`. Sword blocking uses `Vanilla` handling internally, so this toggle controls whether the normal sword slowdown is removed.

Sprint while using an item[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/noslow#sprint-while-using-an-item)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Disable sprint` lets NoSlow briefly cancel sprint when the selected mode needs it. Leave it off if you want NoSlow to preserve sprint whenever the mode allows it.

Related modules[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/noslow#related-modules)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [KillAura](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/killaura)
    

[PreviousLong Jump](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/long-jump)
[NextSpeed](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/speed)

# GUI | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/client/gui.md)
.

Use it to fit the menu to your screen, change what it shows, and keep playing while it is open.

Fit the menu to your screen[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/client/gui#fit-the-menu-to-your-screen)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`GUI scale` chooses `Small`, `Normal`, or `Large`. `Background blur` controls how much of the game behind the menu is blurred. At `0%`, Raven does not blur the background. Higher values make the game less visible.

`Scroll speed` controls how quickly the module list moves. Its value can be set from `10` to `90`.

Keep moving with the menu open[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/client/gui#keep-moving-with-the-menu-open)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Enable `Allow movement` to keep the `WASD` keys active while the GUI is open.

Change the menu appearance[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/client/gui#change-the-menu-appearance)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Dark background` adds a dark layer behind the menu. `Rainbow outlines` gives the GUI outlines rainbow colors. You can also enable `Remove player model` to hide the player preview or `Remove watermark` to hide the Raven watermark.

Keep or reset panel positions[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/client/gui#keep-or-reset-panel-positions)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The GUI groups modules into category panels. Use `Reset positions` to return every panel to its default position. Enable `Save category positions` to keep your panel layout between sessions.

[PreviousIRC](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/client/irc)
[NextSettings](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/client/settings)

# KillAura | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/killaura.md)
.

It chooses targets from the range and filters you set, then uses your rotation and attack timing settings to send each hit. Autoblock is optional.

Finding targets[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/killaura#finding-targets)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Range (attack)` controls how far away a target can be for a hit to land. Vanilla survival reach is `3` blocks. `Range (swing)` can be higher, so the arm can swing at a distance without registering a hit. `Range (block)` controls when an active autoblock mode can start.

`FOV` limits the angle where KillAura can find targets. A value of `360` includes every direction. `Targets` sets how many entities it can track at once. Higher values spread attacks across more targets, so each target is hit less often.

`Sort mode` decides which target is chosen first:

*   `Distance` chooses the closest target. This is the default.
    
*   `Health` chooses the target with the lowest health.
    
*   `Hurt time` chooses the target with the most recent hit timer.
    
*   `Yaw` chooses the target closest to your current look direction.
    

Use `Prioritize enemies` to prefer players on your enemy list. `Ignore teammates` skips players on your team. `Attack players` and `Attack mobs` choose the entity types to include. `Attack fireballs` lets KillAura hit incoming fireballs. `Aim invis` includes invisible players.

Attacks and rotation[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/killaura#attacks-and-rotation)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Timing[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/killaura#timing)

`APS` controls the attack rate. `Autoblock APS` is a separate rate used while autoblock is active. `Attack on damage` waits a number of ticks after you are hit before forcing an attack. Set it to `0` to disable that behavior.

`Switch delay` controls how long KillAura stays on a target before it can switch to another one. A longer delay keeps the target choice steadier. `Require mouse down` limits attacks to the time when you hold the left mouse button.

### Rotation[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/killaura#rotation)

`Rotation mode` controls what you see when KillAura aims:

*   `Silent` sends the rotation to the server while your camera stays where it is.
    
*   `Lock view` turns your camera toward the target.
    
*   `None` keeps your current rotation and attacks in the direction you are already facing.
    

`Rotation smoothing` controls how quickly the rotation reaches the target. `0` turns instantly. Higher values spread the movement over more ticks.

**Legit setup**

Start with `APS` `12`, `Attack on damage` `3`, `Range (attack)` `3`, `Range (swing)` `5`, `Range (block)` `8`, `Rotation mode` `Silent`, `Rotation smoothing` `1`, `Switch delay` `200ms`, and `Targets` `3`.

If you use `Partial` or `Interact A`, try `Autoblock APS` `6`. If your build has `Autoblock lag`, `50%` is a reasonable starting point.

Blocking[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/killaura#blocking)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Autoblock` controls how KillAura uses the sword block action:

*   `Manual` does not autoblock. You block with right-click.
    
*   `Fake` shows the block only on your client and does not block incoming damage.
    
*   `Partial` blocks for a short time between attacks.
    
*   `Vanilla`, `Damage`, and `Swap` are also available, but Raven does not document their precise behavior here.
    
*   `Interact A`, `Interact B`, `Interact C`, and `Interact D` use packet-based interact actions. Raven does not document the exact differences between these modes.
    

With an active autoblock mode, `Manual block` limits blocking to the time when you hold right-click. Without it, autoblock can begin when a target enters `Range (block)`. `Disable while blocking` pauses attacks while you right-click with a sword. `Silent swing while blocking` hides the swing animation on your client only.

`Interact A` can block all incoming hits. That is effective, but it can be obvious when someone is watching the fight.

**Legit setup**

For manual blocking, use `Autoblock` `Manual` with `Disable while blocking` enabled. If you use `Partial` or `Interact A`, enable `Manual block` so the automatic block only runs while you hold right-click.

**Blatant setup**

For `Interact A`, try `Autoblock APS` `10` if the slowdown is acceptable. For `Interact C`, use `Autoblock APS` `4`, set `Attack on damage` to `0`, and pair it with [Hit Select](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/hit-select)
 using `Reset on damage`. Keep `Disable while blocking` and `Hit through blocks` off. If your build has `Autoblock lag`, use `100%` only on a server where lag autoblocks still work.

Current Hypixel checks can mitigate dual autoblock behavior above about `7 APS`, and its lag checks remove the old advantage from lag autoblocks. Start at `6 APS` or lower when testing. Dual modes can also become slower as you lower the APS, so manual blocking may be more reliable. If you use `Interact C`, enable `Sword` in [NoSlow](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/noslow)
.

Conditions[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/killaura#conditions)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Weapon only` limits attacks to the item group configured as weapons in [Client settings](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/client/settings)
. `Disable in inventory` pauses KillAura while an inventory screen is open. `Disable while mining` pauses it while you break a block. Keep both pause settings enabled for normal play.

`Hit through blocks` allows attacks through solid blocks. Strict anti-cheat checks usually reject this, so keep it disabled unless the server allows it.

`Disable sprint midair` changes sprint behavior while you are airborne. Its exact effect is not documented.

Related modules[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/killaura#related-modules)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[Hit Select](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/hit-select)
 can delay KillAura attacks. [WTap](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/wtap)
 resets sprint after a hit. [Reach](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/reach)
 changes attack distance, while [Velocity](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/velocity)
 changes the knockback you receive.

[PreviousJump Reset](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/jump-reset)
[NextReach](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/reach)

# Boost | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/boost.md)
.

It can help you cross a gap or leave a fight quickly. Use `Boost ticks` and `Multiplier` to decide how long the burst lasts and how strong it is.

Where the burst goes[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/boost#where-the-burst-goes)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Boost follows your current movement direction when it runs, so start the burst while moving where you want to go.

Tune the burst[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/boost#tune-the-burst)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Boost ticks` sets how long the extra speed lasts. Higher values keep the burst active longer.

`Multiplier` sets the strength of the velocity burst. A higher value gives more horizontal movement during the selected tick count.

[PreviousBhop](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/bhop)
[NextDolphin](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/dolphin)

### clearTitleText[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/world#cleartitletext)

removes the currently displayed title overlay, including server-sent titles.

Copy

    world.clearTitleText();

* * *

### displayTitleText[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/world#displaytitletext)

displays the vanilla title overlay in the center of the screen.

Copy

    world.displayTitleText(String title, String subtitle, int fadeInTicks, int stayTicks, int fadeOutTicks);

* * *

### exists[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/world#exists)

returns `true` if the world is loaded.

Copy

    boolean exists = world.exists();

* * *

### getBlockAt[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/world#getblockat)

returns a [`Block`](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/block)
 for the specified coordinates or vector.

Copy

    Block blockByPos = world.getBlockAt(Vec3 position);
    Block blockByCoords = world.getBlockAt(int x, int y, int z);

* * *

### getDimension[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/world#getdimension)

returns the current dimension name.

### getEntities[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/world#getentities)

returns every loaded entity.

* * *

### getEntityById[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/world#getentitybyid)

returns an [`Entity`](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/entity)
 by id, or `null` when missing.

* * *

### getMode[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/world#getmode)

returns current gamemode as a string (ex. `NOT_HYPIXEL`, `BEDWARS_PREGAME`, `BEDWARS_GAME` ).

* * *

### getNetworkPlayers[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/world#getnetworkplayers)

returns the network player list.

* * *

### getPlayerEntities[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/world#getplayerentities)

returns all loaded player entities.

* * *

### getScoreboard[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/world#getscoreboard)

returns the sidebar scoreboard lines.

* * *

### getSubtitleText[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/world#getsubtitletext)

returns currently displayed subtitle text or an empty string if no subtitle is visible.

* * *

### getTabFooter[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/world#gettabfooter)

returns the tab list footer text.

* * *

### getTabHeader[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/world#gettabheader)

returns the tab list header text.

* * *

### getTeams[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/world#getteams)

returns a map of team names to their members.

* * *

### getTileEntities[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/world#gettileentities)

returns every loaded tile entity.

* * *

### getTitleText[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/world#gettitletext)

returns currently displayed title text or an empty string if no title is visible.

* * *

### isValidEntity[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/world#isvalidentity)

returns `true` if the provided entity is still loaded.

# render | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/render.md)
.

### block[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/render#block)

renders a block highlight at the specified position.

Copy

    render.block(Vec3 position, int color, boolean outline, boolean shade);
    render.block(int x, int y, int z, int color, boolean outline, boolean shade);

* * *

### bloom[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/render#bloom)

applies a bloom shader to all renders between `prepare` and `apply`.

Copy

    render.bloom.prepare();
    // rendering code
    render.bloom.apply(int passes, float radius);

* * *

### blur[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/render#blur)

applies a blur shader to all renders between `prepare` and `apply`.

Copy

    render.blur.prepare();
    // rendering code
    render.blur.apply(int passes, float radius);

* * *

### drawBeam[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/render#drawbeam)

draws an upwards beam at a position.

* * *

### draw3DPolygon[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/render#draw3dpolygon)

draws a wireframe polygon around a position.

* * *

### entity[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/render#entity)

renders an entity bounding box with optional outline and shade.

* * *

### entityGui[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/render#entitygui)

renders an entity model on the screen.

* * *

### getFontHeight[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/render#getfontheight)

returns the height of the current font renderer.

* * *

### getFontWidth[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/render#getfontwidth)

returns the width of the specified text.

* * *

### getPosition[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/render#getposition)

returns the camera position as a [`Vec3`](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/vec3)
.

* * *

### getRotations[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/render#getrotations)

returns the camera rotations as `[yaw, pitch]`.

* * *

### getThemeRGB[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/render#getthemergb)

returns the RGB color for the specified theme. uses the `Time multiplier` setting for color animation speed.

theme names are the same names returned by [`getSliderString`](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/modules#getsliderstring)
 for theme sliders.

* * *

### image[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/render#image)

renders an [`Image`](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/image)
 on screen.

* * *

### isInView[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/render#isinview)

returns `true` if the given bounding box is within the camera frustum.

* * *

### item[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/render#item)

renders an item stack on screen.

* * *

### line2D[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/render#line2d)

draws a 2d line on screen.

* * *

### line3D[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/render#line3d)

draws a 3d line between two points.

* * *

### rect[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/render#rect)

draws a solid rectangle on screen.

* * *

### roundedRect[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/render#roundedrect)

draws a rounded rectangle on screen.

* * *

### text[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/render#text)

draws text on screen with optional scaling and shadow.

* * *

### tracer[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/render#tracer)

draws a tracer line from the player to an entity or position.

* * *

### worldToScreen[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/render#worldtoscreen)

projects a world position into screen space. returns `null` if the point is not visible.

# AutoClicker | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/autoclicker.md)
.

CPS means clicks per second. The interval changes within that range instead of staying at one fixed delay.

Click rate[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/autoclicker#click-rate)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Min CPS` is the lower limit and `Max CPS` is the upper limit. Keep the two values close when you want a steadier rate. A wider gap creates more variation.

`Jitter` adds small mouse movement to each click. Higher values add more movement. The setting uses values from `0-3`.

Mouse buttons and blocks[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/autoclicker#mouse-buttons-and-blocks)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Enable `Left click` or `Right click` for the button you want the module to press. `Click on damage` makes it left-click when you take damage. Some clients call this behavior `Reduce`.

`Block hit chance` sets how often an attack also starts the sword block action. It requires a sword in your hand. `Weapon only` limits automatic left clicks to weapons, while `Blocks only` limits automatic right clicks to blocks. `Break blocks` lets the left click continue breaking blocks.

Inventory clicking[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/autoclicker#inventory-clicking)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Inventory CPS` controls the click rate while an inventory screen is open. Set it to `0` to stop clicking in inventories.

`Inventory CPS` can help when you refill your hotbar in PotPvP. If you only need automatic block placement, use [FastPlace](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/fastplace)
.

Related modules[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/autoclicker#related-modules)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[ClickAssist](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/clickassist)
 adds clicks to your own input. [Hit Select](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/hit-select)
 can delay clicks from AutoClicker, and [Reduce](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/reduce)
 changes the slowdown after an attack.

If AutoClicker needs to work with the client click delay, see [Delay Remover](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/delay-remover)
.

[Previousworld](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/world)
[NextAimAssist](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/aimassist)

# AimAssist | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/aimassist.md)
.

It can move your camera, or send the assisted rotation to the server while your camera stays where it is. Use the target filters when you only want it to help in certain fights.

Choosing targets[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/aimassist#choosing-targets)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`FOV` limits the angle in front of you where AimAssist can find a target. A value of `360°` includes every direction. `Range` sets the maximum distance.

Use `Ignore teammates` to skip players on your team. `Aim invis` includes invisible players, and `Aim mobs` includes mobs as well as players. `Aim through blocks` allows targets that are behind an obstruction.

### Speed[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/aimassist#speed)

`Speed` controls how quickly the aim moves toward a target. At `100%`, it changes to `Lock` and moves to the target without a delay. Lower values make the movement slower.

**Legit setup**

For help that stays close to your crosshair, try an `FOV` between `80-120°`. Increase it only if AimAssist misses targets that are still near the direction you are facing.

Rotation and input[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/aimassist#rotation-and-input)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Rotate yaw` changes your horizontal rotation. `Rotate pitch` changes your vertical rotation. You can enable either one by itself.

`Silent aim` sends the assisted rotation to the server without changing your view. `Click aim` limits the assistance to the time when you hold the left mouse button. `Stop on target` stops the assistance once your crosshair is already on the target.

Conditions[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/aimassist#conditions)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Stop when breaking` pauses AimAssist while you mine a block. `Weapon only` limits it to the time when you hold a weapon.

Related modules[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/aimassist#related-modules)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

If you want the client to attack for you as well as assist your aim, see [KillAura](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/killaura)
.

[PreviousAutoClicker](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/autoclicker)

# Stasis | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/stasis.md)
.

It repeatedly sends your current position to the server, which can make you appear suspended until you turn the module off.

**Hypixel**

Stasis is patched on Hypixel. Use a pulse value instead of `Disabled` for the current workaround when you need to hold a position briefly.

Control the hold[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/stasis#control-the-hold)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Pulse` sets how often Stasis sends the frozen position. `2 ticks` sends it often, while higher values send it less often.

Hold without pulses[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/stasis#hold-without-pulses)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Set `Pulse` to `Disabled` to hold the position without sending pulses. This is the full hold that the Hypixel warning above refers to.

[PreviousSprint](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/sprint)
[NextStop Motion](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/stop-motion)

# Hitbox | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/hitbox.md)
.

A larger hitbox gives your aim more room, but the change is easy to notice at higher values.

Hit size[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/hitbox#hit-size)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Multiplier` controls the size of the expansion. `1.0x` is the vanilla size. `1.2x` makes the hitbox `20%` larger.

Use `1.1-1.2x` for a small expansion. Higher values make the hitbox visibly oversized and can make hits connect at unusual ranges.

Modified hitboxes are detectable by most anti-cheat systems.

Conditions and display[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/hitbox#conditions-and-display)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Players only` expands player hitboxes but leaves mobs unchanged. It is enabled by default. `Weapon only` applies the expansion only while you hold a weapon.

`Show modified hitbox` draws the expanded hitbox as an outline around the entity. This is visual only.

Related modules[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/hitbox#related-modules)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Use [Reach](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/reach)
 when you need to change attack distance, or [KillAura](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/killaura)
 when you want automatic attacks.

[PreviousClickAssist](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/clickassist)
[NextHit Select](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/hit-select)

Last updated 1 day ago

*   [Hit size](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/hitbox#hit-size)

# TPAura | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/tpaura.md)
.

It changes your position before the attack, so it does not behave like a normal attack from where you are standing.

Moving to a target[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/tpaura#moving-to-a-target)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Range` sets how far away a player can be before TPAura moves to them. `0` disables the module, and the first active value is `0.1`. The setting can reach `30` blocks.

`Weapon only` limits TPAura to the time when you hold a weapon.

Compatibility[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/tpaura#compatibility)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

**Blatant setup**

Other players can see the teleport, and servers with position validation can detect movement that does not follow normal rules. Use TPAura only where this movement is allowed.

Related modules[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/tpaura#related-modules)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[KillAura](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/killaura)
 attacks nearby targets without the direct teleport used by TPAura.

[PreviousReduce](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/reduce)
[NextVelocity](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/velocity)

Last updated 1 day ago

# Reach | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/reach.md)
.

Each swing uses a random distance between `Min` and `Max`, so the module does not use one fixed value every time.

Attack distance[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/reach#attack-distance)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Min` is the shortest distance Reach can use, and `Max` is the longest. Vanilla survival reach is `3.0` blocks. Keep the two values close when you want less variation.

Conditions[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/reach#conditions)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Sprint only` applies the extended reach while you are sprinting. `Moving only` requires movement. `Weapon only` requires a weapon in your hand.

`Hit through blocks` allows attacks through solid blocks. This is similar to what some clients call `Piercing`, and strict anti-cheat checks can reject it.

Related modules[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/reach#related-modules)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[KillAura](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/killaura)
 can use the changed attack distance for automatic attacks. [Hitbox](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/hitbox)
 changes the size of the target instead.

[PreviousKillAura](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/killaura)
[NextReduce](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/reduce)

Last updated 1 day ago

*   [Conditions](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/reach#conditions)

# Fall View | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/fall-view.md)
.

It gives you time to decide whether to keep falling or use another way down.

Fall information[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/fall-view#fall-information)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Show distance` displays the number of blocks you have fallen. `Show damage` displays the estimated damage from the landing. `Show remaining health` displays your estimated health after that damage.

Use `Override health format` when you want the health line to use the custom format instead of the default one.

The damage and remaining-health values are estimates.

When it appears[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/fall-view#when-it-appears)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Disable while flying` hides the display while you use `Fly` or an `Elytra`. `Only while sneaking` shows it only while you hold the sneak key during a fall.

If you want a way to avoid the landing damage, see [Water Bucket](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/water-bucket)
 or [NoFall](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/nofall)
.

[PreviousExtend Camera](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/extend-camera)
[NextHUD](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/hud)

# Speed | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/speed.md)
.

It applies while you walk or strafe, so it is a simpler choice than Bhop when you want faster ground movement.

Choose a movement mode[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/speed#choose-a-movement-mode)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The `Speed` setting has two modes:

*   `Vanilla` multiplies normal walking speed.
    
*   `Float` uses a small floating offset while you move.
    

Choose the multiplier[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/speed#choose-the-multiplier)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Multiplier` sets how much faster you move than normal. `1x` is vanilla speed.

**Legit setup**

Start around `1.1-1.2x` for a small increase. Higher values are more likely to trigger movement checks.

Limit when Speed runs[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/speed#limit-when-speed-runs)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Disable in inventory` pauses Speed while an inventory or container screen is open. `Only forward` applies it only while you hold forward, while `Only strafe` applies it only during sideways movement.

`Setback recovery` lets Speed recover after the server sends a position correction and moves you back.

[PreviousNoSlow](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/noslow)
[NextSprint](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/sprint)

Last updated 1 day ago

It needs a wall where the ladder can be placed, so it is most useful near vertical surfaces.

Start the clutch[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/autoladder#start-the-clutch)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Minimum fall distance` controls how far you must fall before AutoLadder acts. The setting accepts `5` to `40` blocks. Use a higher value if you do not want it to react to small drops.

Choose when it can run[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/autoladder#choose-when-it-can-run)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Only while holding ladder` limits the module to the moments when a ladder is in your hand. This keeps AutoLadder from activating when you are carrying another item.

Other fall saves[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/autoladder#other-fall-saves)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [NoFall](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/nofall)
     changes fall movement data instead of placing a ladder.
    
*   [Water Bucket](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/water-bucket)
     places water during a fall.

# AutoWho | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/minigames/autowho.md)
.

You can choose whether Raven sends the command or uses an artificial result.

Get the player list[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/minigames/autowho#get-the-player-list)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Enable `Artificial` to use Raven's artificial `/who` result instead of sending the command to the server. Enable `Hide message` to keep the `/who` response out of your chat.

Use it in Bed Wars[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/minigames/autowho#use-it-in-bed-wars)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Enable `Run BedWars in game` to run `/who` when a Bed Wars game starts. The command does not work in the Bed Wars pre-game lobby.

# ClickAssist | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/clickassist.md)
.

It keeps your original input and adds the extra clicks alongside it, which raises your effective CPS.

Adding clicks[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/clickassist#adding-clicks)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Enable `Left click` or `Right click` for the button you want to boost. `Chance left` and `Chance right` control how often an extra click is added. At `100%`, the extra click is added every time the matching button is pressed.

Conditions[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/clickassist#conditions)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Weapon only` adds left clicks only while you hold a weapon. `Blocks only` adds right clicks only while you hold a block. `Only while targeting` waits for a target to be in range.

`Above 5 CPS` adds clicks only when your current rate is above `5 CPS`. `Disable in creative` turns the module off in Creative mode. `Disable in replays` turns it off while you watch a replay.

Related modules[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/clickassist#related-modules)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[AutoClicker](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/autoclicker)
 can generate clicks without manual input.

[PreviousAntiKnockback](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/antiknockback)
[NextHitbox](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/hitbox)

Set the interval[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/other/spammer#set-the-interval)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Interval` sets how many game ticks pass between messages. One tick is one game update. The slider accepts `1-40 ticks`; a smaller value sends messages more often.

# Jump Reset | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/jump-reset.md)
.

The jump uses a Minecraft movement mechanic that can partially reduce the knockback you take.

Jump timing[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/jump-reset#jump-timing)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Chance` controls how often Jump Reset jumps after a hit. `Minimize vertical` favors reducing vertical knockback over horizontal knockback.

**Legit setup**

Start with `Chance` at `100%` and enable `Only while targeting`. Leave `Minimize vertical` up to your preference. It favors vertical reduction, while leaving it off gives the normal jump reset behavior.

Conditions[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/jump-reset#conditions)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Only while targeting` runs Jump Reset only when you have a valid target. Turn it off when you want the module to react to every qualifying hit.

Related modules[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/jump-reset#related-modules)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[AntiKnockback](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/antiknockback)
 can delay or cancel selected knockback sources. [Velocity](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/velocity)
 scales the knockback you receive.

[PreviousHit Select](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/hit-select)
[NextKillAura](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/killaura)

# Reduce | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/reduce.md)
.

Use the two controls together when you want to choose both how much slowdown to remove and how often to remove it.

Attack slowdown[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/reduce#attack-slowdown)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Attack reduction` controls the amount removed from each attack. At `100%`, Reduce removes all of the attack slowdown. Lower values leave part of the normal slowdown in place.

**Legit setup**

Try `40-60%` when you want to keep some of the normal movement change. Use `100%` when you want to remove the slowdown completely.

Chance[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/reduce#chance)

---------------------------------------------------------------------

`Chance` controls how often the reduction applies. At `100%`, every attack gets the selected reduction. Lower values let some attacks keep their normal slowdown.

Related modules[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/reduce#related-modules)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[KillAura](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/killaura)
 can use Reduce while it attacks. [Delay Remover](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/delay-remover)
 changes other client delays, while Reduce only changes the slowdown after an attack.

[PreviousReach](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/reach)
[NextTPAura](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/tpaura)

Last updated 1 day ago

# Indicators | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/indicators.md)
.

You can see the direction of a threat without turning your view toward it.

Projectile types[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/indicators#projectile-types)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Choose which projectiles can create an indicator:

*   `Render arrows` shows incoming arrows.
    
*   `Render ender pearls` shows incoming ender pearls.
    
*   `Render fireballs` shows incoming fireballs.
    

Direction and shape[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/indicators#direction-and-shape)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Render only threats` hides projectiles that are not moving toward you. Use `Arrow` to choose the indicator shape:

*   `Caret` uses a `^` shape.
    
*   `Greater than` uses a `>` shape.
    
*   `Triangle` uses a filled triangle.
    

`Circle radius` controls how far the arrows sit from the center of the screen. It accepts values from `30` to `200`.

Enable `Render only threats` if projectiles that are moving away from you make the screen too busy.

Extra detail[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/indicators#extra-detail)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Enable `Item colors` to use the projectile item's color for the indicator. `Render item` adds a small item icon beside the arrow.

`Health bar` adds a health bar beside the highlight.

Set a custom color with `Red / Green / Blue`, using `0-255` for each value. `Rainbow` cycles through hues. `Team color` uses the player's team color instead of the custom color.

Visibility and damage[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/playeresp#visibility-and-damage)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Disable in lobby` hides the highlights in a lobby. `Show invis` includes invisible players. `Render self` applies the highlight to your own model.

Enable `Red on damage` to flash the highlight red when a player takes damage.

# AntiFireball | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/antifireball.md)
.

It is useful in places where a fireball can damage you or push you from a bridge.

Find the fireball[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/antifireball#find-the-fireball)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`FOV` controls the angle that AntiFireball watches. `360` covers every direction. A smaller value, such as `90`, limits the search to the space in front of you. The setting accepts values from `20` to `360`.

`Range` controls how far away a fireball can be before the module reacts. It accepts values from `3` to `20` blocks. A larger range gives the module more time to react, but also makes it watch fireballs that are farther away.

Choose how to deflect[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/antifireball#choose-how-to-deflect)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

When you deflect with a block, `Rotate with blocks` turns you toward the fireball before the interaction. `Rotate with projectiles` does the same when you use a projectile such as a bow. These options help the interaction reach the fireball.

`Silent swing` sends the deflection swing without showing the arm animation.

Pause it when needed[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/antifireball#pause-it-when-needed)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Disable while flying` stops AntiFireball while you are airborne. `Disable while scaffold` stops it while [Scaffold](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/scaffold)
 is active, so a fireball response does not interrupt block placement.

[PreviousAntiAFK](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/antiafk)
[NextAutoBlockin](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/autoblockin)

Last updated 1 day ago

# TargetHUD | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/targethud.md)
.

It can keep the target's name, health, armor, and combat information in one place while you fight.

Panel layout[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/targethud#panel-layout)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Mode` chooses the panel layout:

*   `Modern` uses the compact layout.
    
*   `Legacy` uses the classic layout.
    

Choose `Default`, `Rainbow`, or a named fixed palette with `Theme`. Use `Edit position` to drag the panel to a new place on the screen.

Combat information[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/targethud#combat-information)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Show health` displays the target's current health. `Show difference` displays the health difference between you and the target. `Show win/loss` displays your win/loss record against that player.

`Traditional health color` uses the vanilla color change from green through yellow toward red as health drops.

Target highlight[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/targethud#target-highlight)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Render ESP` also highlights the current target in the world.

TargetHUD follows the current `KillAura` target. See [KillAura](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/killaura)
 for target selection and attack behavior.
