. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/autoblockin.md)
.

Use it when you want cover while you approach or break a bed without placing each block by hand.

Control the placement[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/autoblockin#control-the-placement)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Delay` sets the time between placement attempts. Lower values place more often. A starting value of `0.5 ticks` keeps the delay short without making the placement instant.

Turn on `Only while middle clicking` if you want AutoBlockin to work only while you hold the middle mouse button. `Silent aim` uses the correct placement angle without moving your visible camera.

**Legit setup**

For manual control, use `0.5 ticks` with `Only while middle clicking`, `Silent aim`, `Prioritize stronger blocks`, and `Skip near bed` enabled. This lets you decide when blocking starts while the module handles the placement angle and block choice.

Choose the blocks and positions[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/autoblockin#choose-the-blocks-and-positions)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Prioritize stronger blocks` makes the module choose stronger blocks, such as obsidian or wood, before weaker blocks. `Skip near bed` leaves positions directly beside the target bed open. This can stop the block-in from obstructing the bed.

`Disable obsidian` removes obsidian from the blocks AutoBlockin can place. `Disable in creative` stops the module in Creative mode.

Show placement feedback[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/autoblockin#show-placement-feedback)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Show percentage` displays the block-in progress. `Silent swing` places blocks without showing the arm swing.

Related modules[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/autoblockin#related-modules)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [BedAura](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/bedaura)

# AutoPlace | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/autoplace.md)
.

It also controls the pauses between placement attempts, which helps when a normal held click does not place blocks often enough.

Set the placement timing[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/autoplace#set-the-placement-timing)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Frame delay` adds a wait measured in rendered frames. `0` adds no extra frame delay. Higher values slow the attempts.

`Min place delay` sets the shortest time between two block placements. Lower values place faster. `Min post delay` adds a short pause after a placement before the next attempt begins.

Very low `Min place delay` values can cause the server to reject placements. If blocks stop placing, raise this value. The setting accepts values from `25ms` to `500ms`.

`Motion` changes the movement factor used during placement. `1.0x` is neutral, and other values can change how reliably the block interaction registers.

Decide when it can place[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/autoplace#decide-when-it-can-place)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Hold right` keeps the right-click input held continuously. This is the main behavior of AutoPlace. `Disable left` pauses the module while you hold left-click, which keeps placement from competing with attacks.

`Fast place on jump` uses faster timing while you are jumping. `Pitch check` allows placement only when your vertical look angle is valid for the block interaction.

`Delay until pre` adds a wait before the pre-placement check. It can help when a server needs more time between the check and the placement attempt.

Show the action[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/autoplace#show-the-action)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Show block count` displays how many blocks remain. `Silent swing` places blocks without showing the arm swing.

Related modules[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/autoplace#related-modules)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [Scaffold](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/scaffold)
    
*   [FastPlace](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/fastplace)
    
*   [Tower](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/tower)

# AutoTool | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/autotool.md)
.

It can wait before switching and can return to the hotbar slot you used before the switch.

Choose a tool[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/autotool#choose-a-tool)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Hover delay` sets how long AutoTool waits after you target a block. A short delay keeps the switch quick. A delay of `2` to `3 ticks` gives the change a little time before it happens, while `0` switches without waiting.

Stop unwanted switches[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/autotool#stop-unwanted-switches)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Disable on interactable` prevents tool changes when you target a chest, door, button, or another interactable block. `Disable while holding blocks` pauses the module while you hold a placeable block. `Disable while right click` pauses it during right-click actions.

`Enable when holding tools` makes AutoTool run only when you already hold a tool. `Enable when sneaking` limits it to sneaking. `Require mouse down` changes tools only while you hold the mine button.

Control the slot change[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/autotool#control-the-slot-change)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Spoof item` sends the tool change to the server without changing the item shown in your hand on the client. `Swap to previous slot` returns to the hotbar slot you used before AutoTool selected the tool.

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Enable the projectile types you want to see:

*   `Bow` shows paths for bow shots.
    
*   `Egg` shows paths for thrown eggs.
    
*   `Ender Pearl` shows paths for thrown ender pearls.
    
*   `Snowball` shows paths for thrown snowballs.
    

`Disable uncharged bow` hides the bow path until the bow is fully drawn.

Enable `Disable uncharged bow` if a partial bow charge makes the path hard to read.

Impact markers[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/trajectories#impact-markers)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Highlight on entity` highlights an entity when the predicted path ends on one. `Draw block outline` outlines the block at the predicted impact point.

`Shorten line` makes the path end slightly earlier so the marker is easier to see. `Auto-scale target` changes the endpoint highlight size based on its distance.

. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/autoswap.md)

It keeps a bridge or other build going without a manual slot change.

Choose when to switch[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/autoswap#choose-when-to-switch)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Switch at` sets how many blocks may remain before AutoSwap changes stacks. `0` waits until the current stack is empty. `1` changes one block before empty, which is a practical starting point.

Choose the next stack[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/autoswap#choose-the-next-stack)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Only same type` limits the swap to another stack of the same block type. This prevents a build from changing material when several kinds of blocks are available.

`Swap to greater stack` chooses the matching stack with the most blocks when more than one is available.

Keep the held item unchanged[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/autoswap#keep-the-held-item-unchanged)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Spoof item` sends the slot change to the server without changing the item shown in your hand on the client.

`Players only` shows particles for player hits and hides them for mobs. `Weapon only` shows them only when you attack with a configured weapon. `Disable reach` hides particles for hits made with Reach.

If you use [Reach](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/reach)
, `Disable reach` lets you keep the effect for ordinary hits while hiding it for Reach hits.

# Hit Select | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/hit-select.md)
.

It can apply the delay to your own clicks, AutoClicker, or KillAura. The module changes when an attack is allowed, not which target you aim at.

Choosing a hit window[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/hit-select#choosing-a-hit-window)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Pause` is the amount of time Hit Select can hold an attack after its trigger. Set it to `0ms` to disable the pause. `Only in combo` waits until the required number of consecutive hits before Hit Select starts. `0+` disables that condition.

**Legit setup**

For a simple starting point, use `Pause` `250ms`, `Only in combo` `2+`, and enable `Reset on damage`. This lets the fight settle into a combo before the pause applies and starts the timer again when you are hit.

Critical hit timing[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/hit-select#critical-hit-timing)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Only critical hits` allows attacks on the downward part of a jump. `Only while jumping` limits Hit Select to the time when you are jumping.

If you want the critical hit timing instead of the normal pause, set `Pause` to `0ms`, set `Only in combo` to `1+`, and enable `Only critical hits`.

`Force first hit` allows the opening attack without waiting. `Disable on low health` turns Hit Select off when your health is low.

Applying the delay[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/hit-select#applying-the-delay)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Enable `Manual`, `AutoClicker`, or `KillAura` for the input source you want Hit Select to control. You can enable more than one when you use more than one source.

`Reset on damage` restarts the pause timer when you take damage. `Fake swing` keeps the arm animation visible during a pause. It is cosmetic and does not send a swing to the server.

Related modules[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/hit-select#related-modules)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[AutoClicker](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/autoclicker)
 can provide the clicks that Hit Select filters. [KillAura](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/killaura)
 can use the same timing while it attacks.

[PreviousHitbox](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/hitbox)
[NextJump Reset](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/jump-reset)

# vec3 | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/vec3.md)
.

Vec3[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/vec3#vec3)

----------------------------------------------------------------------

`Vec3` represents a 3d vector with x, y, and z coordinates for position calculations.

property

type

description

x

`double`

x coordinate

y

`double`

y coordinate

z

`double`

z coordinate

### ceil[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/vec3#ceil)

returns a new vector with coordinates rounded up.

Copy

    Vec3 ceiled = position.ceil();

* * *

### equals[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/vec3#equals)

compares two vectors for equality.

Copy

    boolean equal = position.equals(Vec3 other);

* * *

### floor[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/vec3#floor)

returns a new vector with coordinates rounded down.

Copy

    Vec3 floored = position.floor();

* * *

### inverse[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/vec3#inverse)

returns a new vector with negated coordinates.

* * *

### offset[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/vec3#offset)

returns a new vector offset by another vector.

* * *

### offset[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/vec3#offset-1)

returns a new vector offset by values.

* * *

### translate[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/vec3#translate)

returns current vector translated by another vector.

* * *

### translate[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/vec3#translate-1)

returns current vector translated by values.

* * *

### distanceTo[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/vec3#distanceto)

returns the distance to another vector.

* * *

### distanceToSq[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/vec3#distancetosq)

returns the squared distance to another vector.

# WTap | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/wtap.md)
.

This resets the sprint state before a follow-up hit. The timing and conditions decide when the reset happens.

Timing[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/wtap#timing)

-------------------------------------------------------------------

`Delay between reset` controls how long sprint stays released before it is pressed again. `Delay until reset` controls how long WTap waits after the hit before starting that reset.

**Legit setup**

Start with `Delay between reset` at `300ms`, `Delay until reset` at `150ms`, and `Chance` at `100%`. Enable `Players only` and leave `Silent` disabled.

Conditions[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/wtap#conditions)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Chance` controls how often WTap resets sprint after a hit. `Players only` limits the reset to player targets instead of mobs.

`Silent` sends the sprint reset without the visible movement stutter. It can trigger anti-cheat checks, so leave it disabled when you want the less risky option.

Related modules[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/wtap#related-modules)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[KillAura](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/killaura)
 can attack while WTap resets sprint after each hit.

[PreviousVelocity](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/velocity)
[NextAntiShuffle](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/antishuffle)

# BreakProgress | raven client

. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/breakprogress.md)
.

It can also show progress for blocks that `BedAura` is breaking.

Reading the indicator[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/breakprogress#reading-the-indicator)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Choose the display with `Mode`:

*   `Percentage` shows progress from `0-100%`.
    
*   `Seconds` shows the estimated time remaining.
    
*   `Raw` shows the raw break-progress value.
    

`Fade in` makes the indicator fade into view when a break starts.

Choosing what to track[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/breakprogress#choosing-what-to-track)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Enable `Show Manual` for blocks you break yourself. Enable `Show BedAura` for blocks broken by `BedAura`.

If you use `BedAura`, turn on `Show BedAura` to see its block-breaking progress.

See [BedAura](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/bedaura)
 for the module that can break beds automatically.

# Nametags | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/nametags.md)
.

It keeps that information near the player instead of in another screen.

Size and backdrop[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/nametags#size-and-backdrop)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Scale` changes the size of the nametag from `0.1x` to `4x`. `Auto-scale` makes distant nametags smaller.

`Background` controls the backdrop opacity from `0%` to `100%`. Set it to `0%` to remove the backdrop.

Visibility[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/nametags#visibility)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Only render name` hides the extra information and leaves the player's name. `Show invis` includes invisible players. `Sneak suffix` adds an indicator while a player is sneaking.

Use `Render self` to show your own enhanced nametag. `Disable in lobby` hides enhanced nametags outside an active game. `Remove tags` removes clan or guild tags, so `[GUILD] PlayerName` becomes `PlayerName`.

Player details[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/nametags#player-details)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Show distance` adds the distance to the player. `Show health` adds their current health. `Show hits to kill` adds an estimate based on the player's health and your damage.

Equipment details[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/nametags#equipment-details)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Show armor` displays the player's armor. `Show enchants` displays enchantments on their held weapon. `Show durability` displays the remaining durability of the held item, and `Show stack size` displays its stack count.

# BedESP | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/bedesp.md)
.

In Bed Wars, this lets you see a bed and the blocks protecting it before you move in.

Highlight style[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/bedesp#highlight-style)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Choose a color with `Theme`. `Default` uses the standard palette, `Rainbow` cycles through hues, and the other names select fixed palettes.

`Draw beam` adds a beam above each bed. Use it when the bed highlight is hard to spot at a distance. `Render full block` outlines the whole bed instead of only the mattress section.

Finding beds[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/bedesp#finding-beds)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Range` controls how far away the module looks for beds and renders them. It accepts values from `4` to `200` blocks. `Rate` controls how often the scan refreshes, from `0.2` to `5` seconds.

If several beds are visible, `Only render first bed` keeps the highlight on the nearest one. `Optimize for Bed Wars` applies more aggressive filtering to blocks that are not beds.

Bed defenses[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/bedesp#bed-defenses)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Covering blocks` shows the blocks protecting a bed and scales their display from `0.45` to `2x`. Set it to `0.45` to hide covering blocks. `Show whitelisted covering` limits those blocks to entries in the configured whitelist.

Use `Show tools only` when you want the bed display to appear only while you hold a tool. This keeps the covering blocks out of view until you are ready to break them.

Lobby display[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/bedesp#lobby-display)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Enable `Disable in lobby` to hide bed highlights outside an active game.

For automatic bed breaking, see [BedAura](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/bedaura)
.

# AntiAFK | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/antiafk.md)
.

It can move, look around, jump, change the held item, and click at small random intervals.

Move and look[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/antiafk#move-and-look)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`AFK` chooses the movement routine:

*   `Disabled` turns movement off.
    
*   `Shuffle`, `Forward`, `Backward`, and `Wander` select the corresponding movement pattern.
    

`Randomize delta` changes how far each movement step goes. `Randomize pitch` changes the vertical part of the look rotation. Turn on `Spin` if you also want random horizontal look changes.

Add small actions[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/antiafk#add-small-actions)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Jump` adds random jumps to the routine. If you only want jumps when your player is pressed against a wall, enable `Jump only when collided`.

`Swap item` changes the selected hotbar item at random intervals. `Random clicks` sends random left-click actions.

Choose the timing[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/antiafk#choose-the-timing)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Minimum delay ticks` and `Maximum delay ticks` set the shortest and longest wait between actions. AntiAFK chooses a delay between those values, so matching them produces a fixed interval while separating them makes the routine less regular.

[PreviousXray](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/xray)
[NextAntiFireball](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/antifireball)

# FastMine | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/fastmine.md)
.

It can shorten the normal delay without changing the block-crack animation that you see.

Break blocks sooner[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/fastmine#break-blocks-sooner)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Break delay` sets the number of ticks between break packets. Vanilla processes one break packet every `5 ticks`; `1 tick` is a lower, practical setting. Lower values send packets more often.

`Break speed` multiplies the block-breaking speed. `1x` is vanilla speed, while higher values reduce the time needed to break a block.

**Legit setup**

Start with `Break delay: 1 tick` and `Break speed: 1.12x` when you want a lower-speed setup.

`0` for `Break delay` sends packets as fast as possible and can be detected by block-break validation.

Change only the animation[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/fastmine#change-only-the-animation)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Mode` controls when the local crack animation appears:

*   `Pre` shows the crack before the break packet.
    
*   `Post` shows it after the break packet.
    
*   `Increment` increases the crack stage on each tick.
    

This mode changes the animation only. It does not change the actual break speed.

Limit when it runs[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/fastmine#limit-when-it-runs)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Ignore shears` keeps FastMine active while you hold shears. `Disable in creative` stops it in Creative mode.

Related modules[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/fastmine#related-modules)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [FastPlace](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/fastplace)
    
*   [BedAura](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/bedaura)
    

[PreviousDelay Remover](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/delay-remover)
[NextFastPlace](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/fastplace)

# Freecam | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/freecam.md)
.

Your player stays in its server-side position while the camera moves through the world, so you can inspect an area without walking there.

Move the camera[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/freecam#move-the-camera)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Speed` controls how fast the freecam moves. `Mode` changes the movement style:

*   `Default` uses standard freecam movement.
    
*   `Spectator` uses spectator-style movement.
    

Allow actions[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/freecam#allow-actions)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Freecam normally focuses on looking around. `Allow digging` lets you break blocks, `Allow placing` lets you place blocks, and `Allow interacting` lets you use right-click interactions with blocks and entities.

`Allow chatting` lets you send chat messages while Freecam is active.

Show the view and exit[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/freecam#show-the-view-and-exit)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Register attacks[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/delay-remover#register-attacks)

# Fly | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/fly.md)
.

Choose a flight mode, then adjust the horizontal and vertical speed when those controls apply.

Most servers can detect Fly. Use it only where you accept the risk of server action.

Flight mode and speed[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/fly#flight-mode-and-speed)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### `Fly`[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/fly#fly)

The `Fly` setting changes how Fly sends movement data:

*   `Vanilla` uses movement that resembles creative-mode flight.
    
*   `Fast` gives more priority to speed than to stealth.
    

`Horizontal speed` controls movement forward, backward, left, and right. `Vertical speed` controls movement up and down.

HUD and stopping[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/fly#hud-and-stopping)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Show BPS` puts your current blocks per second on the HUD. BPS means blocks per second.

`Stop motion` removes all movement momentum when Fly turns off. Enable it if you do not want to keep drifting after disabling the module.

[PreviousDolphin](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/dolphin)
[NextInvMove](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/invmove)

# Long Jump | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/long-jump.md)
.

It can use a normal jump or a fireball explosion, which gives you another way to cover a large gap.

Create the jump[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/long-jump#create-the-jump)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### `Mode`[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/long-jump#mode)

The `Mode` setting chooses how Long Jump creates the jump:

*   `Vanilla` uses normal jump movement with an added boost.
    
*   `Fireball Auto` fires a fireball below you and uses its explosion knockback.
    

`Horizontal boost` controls the extra movement across the ground plane. `Vertical motion` adds upward movement; `0` keeps the jump flatter.

`Motion ticks` controls how long the boost lasts. `Add motion` adds the boost to your current movement instead of replacing it.

Steering in the air[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/long-jump#steering-in-the-air)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Allow strafe` lets you steer sideways during the jump. `Invert yaw` uses the opposite left-right facing direction for the jump. Yaw is the direction your player faces from left to right.

Starting and stopping[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/long-jump#starting-and-stopping)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Jump` makes Long Jump jump automatically when it activates, so you do not need to press Space.

`Stop motion on disable` removes all movement momentum when Long Jump turns off.

[NextNoSlow](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/noslow)

# AntiCheat | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/other/anticheat.md)
.

A signature is a pattern that may match a cheat. When a pattern matches, AntiCheat can alert you, play a sound, add the player to your enemy list, or send a report.

A flag means that one configured pattern matched. Treat it as an alert to inspect, not proof that the player is cheating.

Choose when to check[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/other/anticheat#choose-when-to-check)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Disable outside minigames` keeps detection active only inside an active minigame. Leave it off if you also want checks in lobbies. `Ignore teammates` skips players on your team.

`Flag interval` sets the minimum time between repeated alerts for one player. It accepts `0-30 seconds`. Use a longer interval when repeated flags become distracting.

Choose how flags reach you[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/other/anticheat#choose-how-flags-reach-you)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Hide chat alerts` removes detection messages from chat. `Should ping` plays a sound when the module flags a player. `Send party chat` forwards the alert to your party chat.

`Add cheaters as enemy` adds flagged players to your enemy list. `Auto report` sends a report to Hypixel through the report function. Keep `Auto report` off if you want to review flags before sending a report.

Choose the signatures[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/other/anticheat#choose-the-signatures)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Each signature has its own toggle. The available checks are `AutoBlock`, `Legit Scaffold`, `NoSlow`, `Nuker`, `Scaffold`, `AimAssist`, `AutoClicker`, `Blink`, `FastMine`, `NoFall`, and `Protocol`.

[PreviousSumo Fences](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/minigames/sumo-fences)
[NextChat Bypass](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/other/chat-bypass)

Last updated 1 day ago

# Blink | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/blink.md)
.

You can keep moving on your screen while the server stays at your last reported position. When Blink releases the queue, the server receives the stored movement and your position catches up. This can make movement look delayed or cause ghosting.

**Hypixel**

Hypixel currently eases burst movement packets in all game modes. Blink no longer gives its old teleport effect there and can cause ghosting. Disable Blink on Hypixel.

Decide when the queue releases[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/blink#decide-when-the-queue-releases)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Auto-disable` releases queued packets after the selected duration. Set it to `0` to keep Blink active until you disable it. The listed values are `0-8 seconds`, but the setting has also been described in ticks. Check the unit shown by the client before choosing a value.

The action toggles release the queue before common state changes:

*   `Disable on attack` releases packets when you attack.
    
*   `Disable on inventory` releases packets when you open an inventory.
    
*   `Disable on mine` releases packets when you break a block.
    
*   `Disable on place` releases packets when you place a block.
    

Handle knockback[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/blink#handle-knockback)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Cancel knockback` discards knockback packets received while Blink is active. The server still applies the knockback, but your client does not show that motion when the queue flushes.

Watch the queue[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/blink#watch-the-queue)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Show blink ticks` displays the queued tick count below your crosshair. `Show initial position` draws a ghost at the position where you enabled Blink.

On a server where packet delay still works, [Fake Lag](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/fake-lag)
 is a better starting point for a manual toggle. It gives you a fixed outbound delay instead of keeping movement queued until you release it.

[PreviousBacktrack](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/backtrack)
[NextFake Lag](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/fake-lag)

Last updated 1 day ago

*   [Decide when the queue releases](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/blink#decide-when-the-queue-releases)
    
*   [Handle knockback](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/blink#handle-knockback)
    
*   [Watch the queue](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/blink#watch-the-queue)

# Latency Alerts | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/other/latency-alerts.md)
.

It sends a chat alert when the measured latency passes the threshold you choose.

Choose when an alert appears[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/other/latency-alerts#choose-when-an-alert-appears)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`High latency` sets the threshold. It accepts `0.1-5 seconds`. Set it above your usual ping so ordinary changes do not trigger an alert.

`Alert interval` sets the minimum time between repeated alerts. It accepts `0-5 seconds`. A longer interval keeps a sustained spike from filling your chat.

Ignore Hypixel Limbo[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/other/latency-alerts#ignore-hypixel-limbo)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Ignore limbo` suppresses alerts while you are in Hypixel Limbo. Turn it on if you do not want Limbo latency included in your alerts.

Control rain[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/world/weather#control-rain)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Swimming speed[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/dolphin#swimming-speed)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Horizontal speed` controls how fast you move across the water. Increase it when you want faster movement across the water.

`Vertical speed` controls how fast you rise or sink. Increase it when you want to change depth faster.

Surface and steering[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/dolphin#surface-and-steering)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Buoyant` moves you toward the surface while you swim forward.

Use `Forward only` when you want the boost to work only while holding forward. Strafe keys by themselves will not activate it.

`Disable vertical while moving` applies the vertical speed only when you are not pressing a movement key. This keeps vertical movement from competing with horizontal strafing underwater.

Using items in water[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/dolphin#using-items-in-water)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Disable while using` pauses Dolphin when you right-click in water. This covers actions such as eating and blocking.

`Range` sets the maximum distance at which TargetStrafe keeps orbiting. The orbit stops when the target moves beyond that distance.

`Only while middle clicking` makes TargetStrafe run only while you hold the middle mouse button. Leave it off when you want it to follow KillAura automatically.

# KeepSprint | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/keepsprint.md)
.

In normal Minecraft, a hit can reduce your sprint speed. KeepSprint lets you keep more of that speed during combat.

Hit slowdown[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/keepsprint#hit-slowdown)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Slow` controls how much of the normal slowdown remains. `0%` removes it completely, while higher values keep more of the slowdown.

**Legit setup**

Start around `20-30%` if you want hits to keep some natural deceleration. Use `0%` when keeping speed matters more than that slowdown.

When the change applies[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/keepsprint#when-the-change-applies)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Disable while jumping` briefly releases sprint during a jump. `Disable sprinting` suppresses the sprint state after a hit instead of restoring it.

`Only reduce reach hits` applies the KeepSprint change only to attacks made with [Reach](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/reach)
. Close-range hits keep their normal sprint slowdown.

[PreviousInvMove](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/invmove)

# Bed Wars | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/minigames/bed-wars.md)
.

It can send alerts to your chat or party chat, play a sound, and show nearby threats on the info HUD.

Choose what to watch[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/minigames/bed-wars#choose-what-to-watch)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Enable `Whitelist own bed` to ignore alerts about your own bed.

`Whitelist own bed` also stops [BedAura](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/bedaura)
 from targeting your own bed.

`Item alerts` contains one toggle for each item group Raven can watch. Each enabled toggle sends an alert when Raven detects that item on an enemy. The groups are `Bow`, `Diamond armor`, `Dream defender`, `Ender pearl`, `Fireball`, `Knockback stick`, `Magic milk`, `Obsidian`, `Potions`, `Rotation items`, and `Water bucket`.

Control alert noise[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/minigames/bed-wars#control-alert-noise)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Alert interval` sets the minimum time between repeated alerts for the same event. Its value can be set from `0` to `30` seconds.

Enable `Invisible players` to receive alerts when Raven detects an invisible player. Enable `Ignore teammates` to skip item alerts for teammates.

Send alerts where you need them[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/minigames/bed-wars#send-alerts-where-you-need-them)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Send party chat` forwards alerts to your party chat. `Should ping` plays a sound whenever an alert fires.

Keep an eye on the bed area[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/minigames/bed-wars#keep-an-eye-on-the-bed-area)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Closest enemy` shows the closest enemy to your bed on the info HUD. Its value can be set from `0` to `5` blocks. Set it to `0` to disable proximity alerts.

Enable `Dragon` to highlight dragons. Enable `Magic milk timer` to show how long Magic Milk remains active.

# HUD | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/hud.md)
.

You can move the list, change how it looks, and remove categories that you do not want to see.

Position and size[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/hud#position-and-size)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Use `Edit position` to enter placement mode and drag the list to a new spot. `Align right` anchors it to the right edge of the screen. `Scale` changes the text size from `0.5x` to `2x`.

Style[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/hud#style)

----------------------------------------------------------------

`Outline` controls the border behind the list:

*   `None` removes the outline.
    
*   `Right` places it on the right side.
    
*   `Left` places it on the left side.
    
*   `Full` surrounds the list on every side.
    

Choose `Default`, `Rainbow`, or a named fixed palette with `Theme`. `Draw background` adds a dark background behind the list.

List content[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/render/hud#list-content)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Alphabetical sort` orders modules by name instead of category. `Lowercase` changes the names to lowercase. `Show module info` adds the active mode or key setting after a module name, such as `KillAura [Single]`.

`Animate` animates modules as they enter or leave the list. Use `Remove closet modules`, `Remove render modules`, and `Remove scripts` to hide those categories from the HUD.

# NoFall | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/nofall.md)
.

The server uses that data to decide whether to apply fall damage. `Minimum fall distance` controls when NoFall starts.

Choose the landing method[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/nofall#choose-the-landing-method)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Mode` changes how NoFall reports the landing:

*   `Packet` sends an explicit on-ground packet.
    
*   `OnGround` sets the on-ground flag in movement packets.
    
*   `Vanilla` delays the packet to match natural landing timing.
    

`Packet` is the recommended starting mode. If the server reacts to it, try `OnGround`.

Decide when it runs[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/nofall#decide-when-it-runs)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Minimum fall distance` sets how far you must fall before NoFall activates. It accepts `0` to `20` blocks.

`Ignore void` leaves fall damage unchanged when you are above the void. It does not prevent void damage.

`Disable adventure` stops NoFall in Adventure mode.

Related modules[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/nofall#related-modules)

# Tower | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/tower.md)
.

It is commonly used with [Scaffold](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/scaffold)
 when you need to bridge and climb.

**Blatant setup**

Use `Mode: Vanilla`, `Slowed ticks: 0`, and enable `Disable SafeWalk`. The speed sliders do not apply in `Vanilla` mode. `Only while right clicking` is optional if Tower activates unreliably.

Choose the tower timing[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/tower#choose-the-tower-timing)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Mode` selects the movement and placement timing:

*   `Vanilla` uses natural jump timing.
    
*   `Normal` uses the standard automated tower.
    
*   `Fly` uses fly-based vertical movement.
    
*   `2 tick`, `2.3 tick`, `2.5 tick`, `4 tick`, `5 tick`, and `6 tick` use tick-based timing variants.
    

`Speed` changes movement speed in non-`Vanilla` modes. `Speed boost` adds upward movement to each jump, also outside `Vanilla`. `Diagonal speed` controls movement speed while towering diagonally.

`Slowed ticks` sets how long Tower stays at reduced speed after a jump. `0` removes that slow phase. `Slowed speed` sets the movement speed during the phase when `Slowed ticks` is above `0`.

Decide when to tower[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/tower#decide-when-to-tower)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Only while right clicking` limits Tower to the moments when you hold right-click. `Disable SafeWalk` stops SafeWalk while Tower is active, which prevents SafeWalk from interfering with tower movement near an edge.

`Disable while collided` pauses Tower when you are pressed against a wall. `Disable in liquid` stops it in water or lava. `Disable while hurt` pauses it after you take damage.

Recover during a climb[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/tower#recover-during-a-climb)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Skip slow with fast scaffold` removes the slow phase while Fast Scaffold is active. `Setback recovery` attempts to continue the tower after the server corrects your position.

Related modules[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/tower#related-modules)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [FastPlace](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/fastplace)
    
*   [SafeWalk](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/safewalk)
    

[PreviousScaffold](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/scaffold)

# FastPlace | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/fastplace.md)
.

It can keep placement limited to blocks and can switch to another stack when the current one runs out.

Set the placement speed[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/fastplace#set-the-placement-speed)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Delay` sets the number of ticks between placements. Vanilla uses `4 ticks`, so `1 tick` makes placement attempts much more frequent.

**Legit setup**

Use `1 tick` with `Blocks only` and `Auto swap blocks` enabled. This keeps FastPlace focused on building and avoids changing slots manually when a block stack is empty.

Limit what it affects[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/fastplace#limit-what-it-affects)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Blocks only` applies FastPlace to blocks instead of other right-click items such as food or bows. `Allow ladders` includes ladders in the faster placement timing.

`Pitch check` requires a downward look angle before FastPlace activates. `Minimum hold time` sets how long you must hold right-click before it activates. A value above `0` ignores short taps.

Keep building without a slot change[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/fastplace#keep-building-without-a-slot-change)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Auto swap blocks` changes to another block stack when the current stack runs out. `Show block count` displays how many blocks remain in the current stack.

Related modules[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/fastplace#related-modules)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

# Timer | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/timer.md)
.

Values above `1x` make client-side movement, attacks, and projectiles happen faster. Values below `1x` make them happen slower.

**Hypixel**

`0x` Timer is patched on Hypixel. Use `0.02x` when you need the slower timer workaround.

Pick a preset[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/timer#pick-a-preset)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Multiplier slider` chooses which configured speed is active: `A`, `B`, or `C`. The preset controls below decide the value for each choice.

Set the preset speeds[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/timer#set-the-preset-speeds)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Slider A` covers values around normal speed. `Slider B` covers slower values. `Slider C` covers higher values.

**Legit setup**

Use `1.1-1.2x` in `Slider A` for a small speed increase without moving far from normal timing.

High Timer values are easy for servers to detect. Use them only where you accept that risk.

Reset and limit Timer[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/timer#reset-and-limit-timer)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

These controls return Timer to `1x` or limit where it applies:

*   `Disable chat & menu` resets Timer while chat or a menu is open.
    
*   `Disable on teleport` resets it when the server sends a teleport packet.
    
*   `Reset on input` resets it when you press a movement key. This supports a burst-then-stop pattern.
    
*   `Strafe only` applies Timer only while you move sideways.
    

[PreviousTeleport](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/teleport)
[NextVClip](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/vclip)

# Bhop | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/bhop.md)
.

Its mode controls the hop timing and any automatic strafing. This matters most when you want to carry speed across several jumps.

Choose how it hops[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/bhop#choose-how-it-hops)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### `Mode`[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/bhop#mode)

The `Mode` setting uses a different timing or strafing pattern for each choice:

*   `Vanilla` follows normal bunny-hop timing.
    
*   `Strafe` adds automatic strafing to keep more speed.
    
*   `Ground` stays on the ground slightly longer between hops.
    
*   `Glide` uses slower, smoother hops for tight spaces.
    
*   `7 tick` and `9 tick` use fixed hop intervals.
    
*   `8 tick strafe` and `9 tick strafe` combine fixed timing with strafing.
    
*   `Hurt time` delays hops around the hurt animation window.
    

Speed and strafing[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/bhop#speed-and-strafing)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Speed` sets the movement cap. Higher values make you move faster while making the movement easier for a server to detect.

**Legit setup**

Start with `1.2-1.4x` if you want a lower profile. Staying below `1.5x` is the safer choice on most anticheat servers.

`Speed boost` adds extra velocity at the start of each hop. `Strafe decay` controls how quickly strafing momentum fades between hops. Lower values keep speed longer, while higher values remove it faster.

`Maximize strafe ticks` uses every available tick for strafe movement. It can keep more speed, but the movement looks less natural.

When Bhop runs[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/bhop#when-bhop-runs)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Auto jump` lets Bhop jump without a Space input. The disable controls keep it from starting in situations where hop timing changes:

*   `Disable in inventory` pauses it while an inventory or container screen is open.
    
*   `Disable in liquid` pauses it in water or lava.
    
*   `Disable with jump potion` pauses it while Jump Boost is active.
    
*   `Disable while collided` pauses it while you press against a wall.
    
*   `Disable while sneaking` pauses it while you hold sneak.
    

Stopping cleanly[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/bhop#stopping-cleanly)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Stop motion on disable` removes horizontal momentum when Bhop turns off. Enable it if you want to stop immediately instead of sliding.

[PreviousWater Bucket](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/player/water-bucket)
[NextBoost](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/movement/boost)

# AntiKnockback | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/antiknockback.md)
.

It works at the packet level, so you can handle different sources separately. Unlike [Velocity](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/velocity)
, it can cancel specific types of knockback on their own.

Knockback amount[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/antiknockback#knockback-amount)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Horizontal` and `Vertical` control how much knockback you take on each axis. Lower values keep less of the incoming movement. `0%` cancels that axis.

`Damage boost` changes your own movement after you take damage. The boost is disabled at `0.8x`. `Boost with LMB` applies it only while you hold the left mouse button.

Delaying a hit[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/antiknockback#delaying-a-hit)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Delay` holds knockback for the selected number of milliseconds after you are hit. `0` disables the delay. Use `Delay only midair` or `Delay only targeting` when the delay should apply only in those situations.

`Delay simultaneously` applies the delay and the cancellation at the same time. With `Delay until ground`, knockback waits until you touch the ground. This can be useful with [Jump Reset](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/jump-reset)
.

`Disable attack while delay` prevents attacks during the delay window. If your latency is low, leave it enabled so you do not attack while the knockback is waiting.

`Delay until ground` holds the knockback long enough for [Jump Reset](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/jump-reset)
 to act before you land.

Cancellation conditions[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/antiknockback#cancellation-conditions)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The cancellation toggles decide when AntiKnockback removes knockback:

*   `Cancel ground` applies it while you are on the ground.
    
*   `Cancel off ground` applies it while you are airborne.
    
*   `Cancel burning` handles knockback while you are on fire.
    
*   `Cancel explosion` handles explosions and TNT.
    
*   `Cancel while falling` handles knockback while you are falling.
    

`Only while moving` limits cancellation to the time when you are moving. `Cancel interval` controls how often cancellation runs. `1` runs it every tick, while the minimum value disables cancellation.

`Disable in lobby` turns AntiKnockback off automatically in lobbies.

Related modules[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/antiknockback#related-modules)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Use [Velocity](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/velocity)
 when you want to scale knockback with a smaller set of conditions. Use [Jump Reset](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/jump-reset)
 when you want to change knockback with a jump.

[PreviousAimAssist](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/aimassist)
[NextClickAssist](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/clickassist)

Last updated 1 day ago

*   [Knockback amount](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/antiknockback#knockback-amount)
    
*   [Delaying a hit](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/antiknockback#delaying-a-hit)

# Fake Lag | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/fake-lag.md)
.

You continue to see your movement locally, while the server receives it after `Outbound delay`. With a pulse, the module releases queued packets in bursts. This is easier to control than Blink when you want to toggle a fixed delay.

**Hypixel**

Hypixel currently eases burst movement packets in all game modes. Fake Lag no longer gives its old teleport or hit advantage there and can cause ghosting. Disable Fake Lag on Hypixel.

Set the delay[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/fake-lag#set-the-delay)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Outbound delay` sets how long outgoing packets wait. The slider accepts `20-1600 ms`.

`Pulse` sets the interval between packet bursts. Use `120-1200 ms` for a pulse, or set it to `0` for a continuous delay.

**Starting point**

On a server where this delay still has an effect, start with `Outbound delay` at `600 ms` and `Pulse` disabled. Fake Lag is the better starting point when you want a manual delay. It does not provide the old Hypixel advantage.

Release before an attack[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/fake-lag#release-before-an-attack)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Disable on attack` releases all queued packets when you attack. This lets the server receive your recent movement before it processes the attack.

`Show server position` draws a ghost at your real server position while Fake Lag is active. Use it to see how far your local position has moved ahead of the server.

If you want to hold movement until a manual release instead, see [Blink](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/blink)
. If you want to buffer nearby players instead, see [Backtrack](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/backtrack)
.

[PreviousBlink](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/blink)
[NextFreeze](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/freeze)

Last updated 1 day ago

*   [Set the delay](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/network/fake-lag#set-the-delay)

# Velocity | raven client

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/velocity.md)
.

It scales the horizontal and vertical movement and can limit the change to selected hits.

Knockback amount[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/velocity#knockback-amount)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Horizontal` and `Vertical` set the percentage of knockback to keep. `100%` is the vanilla amount. `0%` cancels the selected axis.

`Chance` controls how often the reduction applies. At `100%`, every qualifying hit uses the selected horizontal and vertical values. Lower values let some hits use normal knockback.

**Legit setup**

Values between `80-95%` reduce knockback while keeping part of the vanilla response.

Conditions[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/velocity#conditions)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`Only while targeting` reduces knockback only when a target is in your field of view. `Disable while holding S` restores full knockback while you hold the backwards key, which lets you take normal knockback when you need to reposition.

Servers that check player movement can detect changes to knockback. Use [AntiKnockback](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/antiknockback)
 or [Jump Reset](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/jump-reset)
 when you need different knockback conditions.

Related modules[](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/velocity#related-modules)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[AntiKnockback](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/antiknockback)
 can handle separate knockback sources. [Jump Reset](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/jump-reset)
 changes knockback with a jump after you are hit.

[PreviousTPAura](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/tpaura)
[NextWTap](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/wtap)

Last updated 1 day ago

*   [Knockback amount](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/velocity#knockback-amount)
    
*   [Conditions](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/velocity#conditions)

# block

## Block

\`Block\` represents a block in the world with its properties and metadata.

| property     | type      | description                                |
| ------------ | --------- | ------------------------------------------ |
| type         | \`String\`  | block class name                           |
| name         | \`String\`  | registry name                              |
| variant      | \`int\`     | block metadata                             |
| interactable | \`boolean\` | \`true\` if the block can be interacted with |
| height       | \`double\`  | block bounding height                      |
| width        | \`double\`  | block bounding width                       |
| length       | \`double\`  | block bounding length                      |

# bridge

### add

adds or updates a value in the shared bridge map.

\`\`\`java
bridge.add(String key, Object value);
bridge.add(String key);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### clear

removes every entry stored in the bridge map.

\`\`\`java
bridge.clear();
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### get

returns the value stored under a specified key, or \`null\` if it does not exist.

\`\`\`java
Object value = bridge.get(String key);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### has

returns \`true\` if the bridge map contains the specified key.

\`\`\`java
boolean hasKey = bridge.has(String key);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### remove

removes a key and its associated value from the bridge map.

\`\`\`java
bridge.remove(String key);
\`\`\`

Image image = new Image(String url, boolean cache);

# Unknown

\> For the complete documentation index, see \[llms.txt\](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt). Markdown versions of documentation pages are available by appending \`.md\` to page URLs; this page is available as \[Markdown\](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/json.md).

# json

## Json

\`Json\` represents parsed json data. see sections below for available helpers.

### constructor

creates a new json object from a string.

\`\`\`java
Json json = new Json(String contents);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### object

returns the json object itself or a named member as a new \`Json\` instance.

\`\`\`java
Json obj = json.object();
Json member = json.object(String memberName);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### array

returns the json array or a named member as a list of \`Json\` objects.

\`\`\`java
List<Json> array = json.array();
List<Json> memberArray = json.array(String memberName);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### map

returns a map of object members to \`Json\` values.

\`\`\`java
Map<String, Json> members = json.map();
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### get

returns the string value of a member, optionally providing a default.

\`\`\`java
String value = json.get(String memberName);
String valueOrDefault = json.get(String memberName, String defaultValue);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### exists

returns \`true\` if the json value is not null.

\`\`\`java
boolean exists = json.exists();
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### equals

compares two json values for equality.

\`\`\`java
boolean same = json.equals(Json other);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### string

returns the json value as a string. non-primitive values are serialized.

\`\`\`java
String contents = json.string();
\`\`\`

# itemstack

## ItemStack

\`ItemStack\` represents an item with its properties, durability, and metadata.

| property      | type      | description                   |
| ------------- | --------- | ----------------------------- |
| type          | \`String\`  | item class name               |
| name          | \`String\`  | registry name                 |
| displayName   | \`String\`  | display name                  |
| stackSize     | \`int\`     | current stack size            |
| maxStackSize  | \`int\`     | max stack size                |
| durability    | \`int\`     | remaining durability          |
| maxDurability | \`int\`     | max durability                |
| isBlock       | \`boolean\` | \`true\` if the item is a block |
| meta          | \`int\`     | item metadata                 |

### constructor

creates a new item stack from a registry name.

\`\`\`java
ItemStack stack = new ItemStack(String name);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### getEnchantments

returns a list of enchantments on the item.

\`\`\`java
List<Object\[\]> enchantments = stack.getEnchantments();
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### getTooltip

returns the item's tooltip as a list of strings.

\`\`\`java
List<String> tooltip = stack.getTooltip();
\`\`\`

{% hint style="info" %}

\*\*\*

### getBookContents

\*\*\*

returns the chest name when a chest is open, otherwise an empty string.

\*\*\*

returns the size of the opened chest inventory, or \`0\` if no chest is open.

\*\*\*

### getContainer

returns the simple class name of the currently open container.

\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### getSize

returns the size of the player's inventory.

\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### getSlot

returns the currently selected hotbar slot index.

\`\`\`

\*\*\*

returns the item in the specified chest slot as an \[\`ItemStack\`\](/scripting/documentation/itemstack.md), or \`null\` when unavailable.

\`\`\`java

\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### getStackInSlot

returns the item in the specified player inventory slot as an \[\`ItemStack\`\](/scripting/documentation/itemstack.md).

\`\`\`java
ItemStack item = inventory.getStackInSlot(int slot);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### open

simulates opening the inventory gui.

\`\`\`java

\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### setSlot

sets the currently selected hotbar slot.

\`\`\`java

\`\`\`

# Unknown

\> For the complete documentation index, see \[llms.txt\](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt). Markdown versions of documentation pages are available by appending \`.md\` to page URLs; this page is available as \[Markdown\](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/documentation/loadstring.md).

# load string

Must be on a separate line in the following format:

\`\`\`java
load - "URL"
\`\`\`

{% hint style="warning" %}
Loaded content will replace the entire script source code.&#x20;

Nested load strings are not supported.
{% endhint %}

\*\*\*

### Example

Here is an example script hosted on \[Pastebin\](https://pastebin.com/93hWSJeN):

\`\`\`java
void onEnable() {
    client.print("enabled script!");
}
\`\`\`

You can load it using the Pastebin raw link:

\`\`\`java
load - "https://pastebin.com/raw/93hWSJeN"
\`\`\`

Alternatively, load from a local directory:

\`\`\`java
load - "file:///C:/Users/user/Desktop/script.txt"
\`\`\`

---

returns \`true\` if the specified mouse button is pressed. use \`0\` for left, \`1\` for right, and \`2\` for middle click.

\*\*\*

### setPressed

\`\`\`

# gl

{% hint style="warning" %}
advanced rendering api - improper use may cause visual glitches. use \[\`render\`\](/scripting/documentation/render.md) methods when possible.
{% endhint %}

### alpha

enables or disables alpha testing.

\`\`\`java
gl.alpha(boolean enabled);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### begin

starts specifying vertices for a primitive shape.

\`\`\`java
gl.begin(int mode);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### blend

enables or disables blending.

\`\`\`java
gl.blend(boolean enabled);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### color

sets the current drawing color.

\`\`\`java
gl.color(int r, int g, int b, int a);
gl.color(double r, double g, double b, double a);
gl.color(float r, float g, float b, float a);
gl.color(int rgb);
gl.resetColor(); // set to white
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### cull

enables or disables face culling.

\`\`\`java
gl.cull(boolean enabled);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### depth

enables or disables depth testing.

\`\`\`java
gl.depth(boolean enabled);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### depthMask

sets whether writing to the depth buffer is enabled.

\`\`\`java
gl.depthMask(boolean enabled);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### disable

disables an opengl capability.

\`\`\`java
gl.disable(int cap);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### disableItemLighting

disables standard item lighting.

\`\`\`java
gl.disableItemLighting();
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### enable

enables an opengl capability.

\`\`\`java
gl.enable(int cap);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### enableItemLighting

enables standard item lighting. set \`gui\` to \`true\` when rendering in guis.

\`\`\`java
gl.enableItemLighting(boolean gui);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### end

completes the current vertex specification.

\`\`\`java
gl.end();
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### lighting

enables or disables lighting.

\`\`\`java
gl.lighting(boolean enabled);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### lineSmooth

enables or disables line smoothing.

\`\`\`java
gl.lineSmooth(boolean enabled);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### lineWidth

sets the width of lines.

\`\`\`java
gl.lineWidth(double width);
gl.lineWidth(float width);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### normal

specifies a normal vector for lighting calculations.

\`\`\`java
gl.normal(double x, double y, double z);
gl.normal(float x, float y, float z);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### pop

pops the current matrix stack.

\`\`\`java
gl.pop();
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### push

pushes the current matrix stack.

\`\`\`java
gl.push();
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### rotate

applies rotation around the specified axis.

\`\`\`java
gl.rotate(double angle, double x, double y, double z);
gl.rotate(float angle, float x, float y, float z);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### scale

scales the current transformation matrix.

\`\`\`java
gl.scale(double x, double y, double z);
gl.scale(float x, float y, float z);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### scissor (box)

sets the scissor rectangle.

\`\`\`java
gl.scissor(int x, int y, int width, int height);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### scissor (toggle)

enables or disables scissoring.

\`\`\`java
gl.scissor(boolean enabled);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### texture2d

enables or disables 2d texturing.

\`\`\`java
gl.texture2d(boolean enabled);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### translate

translates the current transformation matrix.

\`\`\`java
gl.translate(double x, double y, double z);
gl.translate(float x, float y, float z);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### vertex2

specifies a 2d vertex.

\`\`\`java
gl.vertex2(double x, double y);
gl.vertex2(float x, float y);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### vertex3

specifies a 3d vertex.

\`\`\`java
gl.vertex3(double x, double y, double z);
gl.vertex3(float x, float y, float z);
\`\`\`

# Unknown

\> For the complete documentation index, see \[llms.txt\](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt). # playerstate

## PlayerState

\`PlayerState\` represents a player's position, rotation, and movement state.

| property    | type      | description                     |
| ----------- | --------- | ------------------------------- |
| x           | \`double\`  | x coordinate                    |
| y           | \`double\`  | y coordinate                    |
| z           | \`double\`  | z coordinate                    |
| yaw         | \`float\`   | yaw rotation                    |
| pitch       | \`float\`   | pitch rotation                  |
| onGround    | \`boolean\` | whether the player is on ground |
| isSprinting | \`boolean\` | whether the player is sprinting |
| isSneaking  | \`boolean\` | whether the player is sneaking  |

### constructor

creates a new player state with individual parameters.

\`\`\`java
PlayerState state = new PlayerState(double x, double y, double z, float yaw, float pitch, boolean onGround, boolean sprinting, boolean sneaking);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### constructor

creates a new player state from an array of data.

\`\`\`java
PlayerState state = new PlayerState(Object\[\] data);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### asArray

returns the player state data as an object array.

\`\`\`java
Object\[\] data = state.asArray();
\`\`\`

\> For the complete documentation index, see \[llms.txt\](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt). # request

## Request

\`Request\` represents an http request with methods, headers, and content.

### constructor

creates a new request to a specified url. supported methods: \`GET\`, \`POST\`.

\`\`\`java
Request request = new Request(String method, String url);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### addHeader

adds a header to the request.

\`\`\`java
request.addHeader(String header, String value);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### setUserAgent

sets the user-agent header for the request.

\`\`\`java
request.setUserAgent(String userAgent);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### setConnectTimeout

sets the connection timeout in milliseconds.

\`\`\`java
request.setConnectTimeout(int timeout);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### setReadTimeout

sets the read timeout in milliseconds.

\`\`\`java
request.setReadTimeout(int timeout);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### setContent

sets the content to be sent with a post request.

\`\`\`java
request.setContent(String content);
\`\`\`

\*\*\*

### fetch

executes the request and returns a \[\`Response\`\](/scripting/documentation/response.md).

\`\`\`java
Response response = request.fetch();
\`\`\`

# Unknown

\> For the complete documentation index, see \[llms.txt\](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/llms.txt). Markdown versions of documentation pages are available by appending \`.md\` to page URLs; this page is available as \[Markdown\](https://daikirai.gitbook.io/scripting/combat/velocity.md).

# Velocity

It scales the horizontal and vertical movement and can limit the change to selected hits.

## Knockback amount

\`Horizontal\` and \`Vertical\` set the percentage of knockback to keep. \`100%\` is the vanilla amount. \`0%\` cancels the selected axis.

\`Chance\` controls how often the reduction applies. At \`100%\`, every qualifying hit uses the selected horizontal and vertical values. Lower values let some hits use normal knockback.

{% hint style="success" %}
\*\*Legit setup\*\*

Values between \`80-95%\` reduce knockback while keeping part of the vanilla response.
{% endhint %}

## Conditions

\`Only while targeting\` reduces knockback only when a target is in your field of view. \`Disable while holding S\` restores full knockback while you hold the backwards key, which lets you take normal knockback when you need to reposition.

{% hint style="warning" %}
Servers that check player movement can detect changes to knockback. Use \[AntiKnockback\](/scripting/combat/antiknockback.md) or \[Jump Reset\](/scripting/combat/jump-reset.md) when you need different knockback conditions.
{% endhint %}

## Related modules

\[AntiKnockback\](/scripting/combat/antiknockback.md) can handle separate knockback sources. \[Jump Reset\](/scripting/combat/jump-reset.md) changes knockback with a jump after you are hit.

---

# Unknown
